Fact → String)\n    (result : Step State Outcome Fact) : String :=\n  let named := fun (kind key : String) => ModelValue.named (origin.ownedId kind owner key) key\n  stepOrderKey ⟨named \"outcome\" (outcomeKey result.outcome),\n    named \"state\" (stateKey result.state),\n    result.facts.map fun fact => named \"fact\" (factKey fact)⟩\n\n/-- Insert one result before the first it does not follow.\n\nStructural rather than `List.mergeSort`, whose well-founded recursion does not unfold: a machine's\ncanonical-table law is proved by `rfl` over the enumerated rows, so every function a row is computed\nby has to reduce. -/\ndef insertOrdered (key : α → String) (item : α) : List α → List α\n  | [] => [item]\n  | head :: rest =>\n      if key item ≤ key head then item :: head :: rest else head :: insertOrdered key item rest\n\n/-- An extended row: the source's results and the author's, in the order Search admits them. -/\ndef extendResults (origin : Origin) (owner : String)\n    (stateKey : State → String) (outcomeKey : Outcome → String) (factKey : Fact → String)\n    (source extra : List (Step State Outcome Fact)) : List (Step State Outcome Fact) :=\n  let key := resultOrderKey origin owner stateKey outcomeKey factKey\n  (source ++ extra).foldr (insertOrdered key) []\n\n/-- Why an extension is refused, at the state and action member it is refused at. -/\ninductive ExtendConflict where\n  /-- The source has no row for the action here, so the extension would enable it. -/\n  | disabledSource (state action : String)\n  /-- An extension result the row already holds, or that the extension returns twice. -/\n  | duplicate (state action : String)\n  deriving BEq, DecidableEq, Repr\n\n/-- The conflict as the elaborator reads it back: its kind, then the state and action keys. -/\ndef ExtendConflict.fields : ExtendConflict → List String\n  | .disabledSource state action => [\"disabledSource\", state, action]\n  | .duplicate state action => [\"duplicate\", state, action]\n\n/-- The first conflict an extension has over every state and every member of the extended action,\nin the states' and actions' catalog order. Results are compared by their keys, which is what the\nemitted table and the Behavior Fingerprint read. -/\ndef firstConflict (states : List State) (actions : List Action)\n    (stateKey : State → String) (actionKey : Action → String)\n    (outcomeKey : Outcome → String) (factKey : Fact → String)\n    (source extra : State → Action → List (Step State Outcome Fact)) : Option ExtendConflict :=\n  let resultKey := fun (result : Step State Outcome Fact) =>\n    (outcomeKey result.outcome, stateKey result.state, result.facts.map factKey)\n  states.findSome? fun state => actions.findSome? fun taken =>\n    let held := source state taken\n    let added := extra state taken\n    if added.isEmpty then none\n    else if held.isEmpty then some (.disabledSource (stateKey state) (actionKey taken))\n    else\n      let heldKeys := held.map resultKey\n      let addedKeys := added.map resultKey\n      if addedKeys.any heldKeys.contains || addedKeys.eraseDups.length != addedKeys.length then\n        some (.duplicate (stateKey state) (actionKey taken))\n      else none\n\nend Umpire.Command.Derived\n    set_option maxHeartbeats 1000000 in\n    $generated:command))\n\n/-- What one `steps:` line resolved to: the action it steps on, that action's input domains, and the\nfunction the author wrote. -/\nprivate structure ResolvedStep where\n  action : Registry.ActionEntry\n  /-- Each input field's domain type, in declaration order: the function's arguments after the\n  state, curried in that order. -/\n  domains : Array Name\n  function : Ident\n\n/-- The Outcome and Fact domains a step function returns, read off its own type.\n\nThey are not keys the author writes. A step function's result type is\n`List (Step State Outcome Fact)`, so the machine's outcome and fact domains are already written down\nin the function the author wrote; asking for them again would be asking twice and admitting the\nanswers to disagree. -/\nprivate def stepResultDomains (stepRef : Ident) (declName stateDecl : Name)\n    (domains : Array Name) : CommandElabM (Name × Name) := do\n  let arity := domains.size\n  let some info := (← getEnv).find? declName\n    | throwErrorAt stepRef (stepSignatureMessage declName)\n  liftTermElabM do\n    Meta.forallBoundedTelescope info.type (some (arity + 1)) fun taken result => do\n      -- `forallBoundedTelescope` takes *at most* the bound, so a function with fewer arguments than\n      -- the action declares inputs would reach the unification below with a function type and fail\n      -- somewhere inside the synthesized dispatcher instead of here, at the line that named it.\n      unless taken.size == arity + 1 do\n        throwErrorAt stepRef (stepSignatureMessage declName)\n      -- The first argument is the state. Without this a function over another type reaches the\n      -- dispatcher, fails there, and is then reported by the axiom guard as a machine too large to\n      -- prove -- a verdict about size on what is a type error.\n      let carriedState ← Meta.inferType taken[0]!\n      let expectedState ← mkConstWithLevelParams stateDecl\n      unless (← Meta.isDefEq carriedState expectedState) do\n        throwErrorAt stepRef (stepStateMessage declName carriedState expectedState)\n      -- Each argument after it is the action's own input domain, in order. Two actions of the same\n      -- arity over different enums are an ordinary slip, and without this the mismatch surfaces\n      -- inside the synthesized dispatcher rather than at the line that named the function.\n      for index in [0:domains.size] do\n        let carried ← Meta.inferType taken[index + 1]!\n        let expected ← mkConstWithLevelParams domains[index]!\n        unless (← Meta.isDefEq carried expected) do\n          throwErrorAt stepRef (stepArgumentMessage declName index domains[index]! carried)\n      -- Unified against the shape rather than matched on the head constant: `Umpire.Step` is an\n      -- abbreviation, so a match on what it reduces to would name a type the author never wrote,\n      -- and would break the day the abbreviation moves.\n      let stateType ← mkConstWithLevelParams stateDecl\n      -- A Model's domains are all `Type`, so the holes are `Type` holes; a `Sort ?u` hole unifies\n      -- with the universe rather than with the domain and reports the mismatch in `Step`'s own\n      -- application, where an author cannot see what went wrong.\n      let anyType := Expr.sort (Level.succ Level.zero)\n      let outcome ← Meta.mkFreshExprMVar anyType\n      let fact ← Meta.mkFreshExprMVar anyType\n      let expected ← Meta.mkAppM ``List #[← Meta.mkAppM ``Umpire.Step #[stateType, outcome, fact]]\n      unless (← Meta.isDefEq result expected) do\n        throwErrorAt stepRef (stepSignatureMessage declName)\n      let some outcomeName := (← instantiateMVars outcome).getAppFn.constName?\n        | throwErrorAt stepRef (stepSignatureMessage declName)\n      let some factName := (← instantiateMVars fact).getAppFn.constName?\n        | throwErrorAt stepRef (stepSignatureMessage declName)\n      pure (outcomeName, factName)\n\n/-- What a `from:` line derives a machine from: the source's declarations, the steps the derived\nmachine keeps, each over a function generated for it, and the timers it keeps. -/\nprivate structure Derivation where\n  source : Registry.MachineEntry\n  sourceModel : Registry.ModelEntry\n  entity : Registry.EntityEntry\n  steps : Array ResolvedStep\n  timers : Array String\n\n/-- Resolve a derived machine's `from:`, `restrict:` and `extend:` lines and generate one step\nfunction per action it keeps.\n\nEach generated function steps the source's own dispatcher on the source's own action, so a step\nfunction the source module keeps private is still reached, and an extended action's function adds\nthe author's results to it in the order Search admits them. An extension is checked against the\nsource over every state and every class of its action before anything reads the derived table:\na result where the source has no row, or one the row already holds, is refused at its `extend:`\nline. -/\nprivate def deriveMachine (name sourceRef : Ident) (restrictRefs : Array Ident)\n    (extendRefs : Array (Ident × Ident)) : CommandElabM Derivation := do\n  let env ← getEnv\n  let declName? ← try\n      some <$> liftTermElabM (realizeGlobalConstNoOverloadWithInfo sourceRef)\n    catch failure =>\n      if failure.isInterrupt || failure.isMaxRecDepth then throw failure else pure none\n  let some source := declName?.bind (Registry.machine? env)\n    | throwErrorAt sourceRef (undeclaredSourceMessage sourceRef.getId)\n  let some sourceModel := Registry.model? env source.declName\n    | throwErrorAt sourceRef (undeclaredSourceMessage sourceRef.getId)\n  let some entity := Registry.entity? env source.entity\n    | throwErrorAt sourceRef (undeclaredSourceMessage sourceRef.getId)\n  -- The source's steps in its own order, each with its action's inputs: a timer has none and no\n  -- declaration, the way the source recorded it.\n  let sourceSteps ← source.steps.mapM fun (spelling, _) => do\n    if source.timers.contains spelling then\n      pure ({ declName := .anonymous, name := spelling, party := \"system\", subject := none\n              inputFields := #[], results := none } : Registry.ActionEntry)\n    else\n      let some declared := source.actionDecls.findSome? fun actionDecl =>\n          (Registry.action? env actionDecl).filter (·.name == spelling)\n        | throwErrorAt sourceRef (undeclaredSourceMessage sourceRef.getId)\n      pure declared\n  let stepped := \", \".intercalate (sourceSteps.toList.map fun entry => s!\"'{entry.name}'\")\n  -- `restrict:` applies first, so an `extend:` line names what the restriction kept.\n  let mut kept := sourceSteps\n  unless restrictRefs.isEmpty do\n    let mut listed : Array String := #[]\n    for restrictRef in restrictRefs do\n      let spelling := restrictRef.getId.getString!\n      if source.timers.contains spelling then\n        throwErrorAt restrictRef (restrictTimerMessage spelling)\n      unless sourceSteps.any (·.name == spelling) do\n        throwErrorAt restrictRef (absentActionMessage \"restrict:\" spelling stepped)\n      if listed.contains spelling then\n        throwErrorAt restrictRef (duplicateDerivationMessage \"restrict:\" spelling)\n      listed := listed.push spelling\n    kept := sourceSteps.filter fun entry => listed.contains entry.name\n  let stateIdent := mkIdentFrom sourceRef (`_root_ ++ source.stateType)\n  let outcomeIdent := mkIdentFrom sourceRef (`_root_ ++ sourceModel.outcomeType)\n  let factIdent := mkIdentFrom sourceRef (`_root_ ++ sourceModel.factType)\n  let sourceIdent := fun (suffix : Name) =>\n    mkIdentFrom sourceRef (`_root_ ++ source.declName ++ suffix)\n  let mut extensions : Array (String × Ident × Ident) := #[]\n  for (actionRef, functionRef) in extendRefs do\n    let spelling := actionRef.getId.getString!\n    let some entry := kept.find? (·.name == spelling)\n      | throwErrorAt actionRef (absentActionMessage \"extend:\" spelling\n          (\", \".intercalate (kept.toList.map fun entry => s!\"'{entry.name}'\")))\n    if extensions.any (·.1 == spelling) then\n      throwErrorAt actionRef (duplicateDerivationMessage \"extend:\" spelling)\n    let functionDecl ← liftTermElabM (realizeGlobalConstNoOverloadWithInfo functionRef)\n    let found ← stepResultDomains functionRef functionDecl source.stateType\n      (entry.inputFields.map Prod.snd)\n    unless found == (sourceModel.outcomeType, sourceModel.factType) do\n      throwErrorAt functionRef (disagreeingDomainsMessage sourceModel.outcomeType\n        sourceModel.factType found.1 found.2)\n    extensions := extensions.push (spelling, actionRef, functionRef)\n  let stateBinder := mkIdent `state\n  let carriedOf := fun (entry : Registry.ActionEntry) =>\n    (List.range entry.inputFields.size).toArray.map fun index =>\n      mkIdent (Name.mkSimple s!\"carried{index}\")\n  let origin ← originTerm\n  let owner := Lean.quote name.getId.toString\n  let mut steps : Array ResolvedStep := #[]\n  for entry in kept do\n    let carried := carriedOf entry\n    let taken := Syntax.mkApp (sourceIdent (`Action ++ Name.mkSimple entry.name)) carried\n    let inherited ← `(term| $(sourceIdent `step) $stateBinder ($taken))\n    let body ← match extensions.find? (·.1 == entry.name) with\n      | none => pure inherited\n      | some (_, _, functionRef) =>\n          `(term| Umpire.Command.Derived.extendResults $origin $owner\n            $(sourceIdent `stateKeyFor) $(sourceIdent `outcomeKeyFor) $(sourceIdent `factKeyFor)\n            ($inherited) ($(Syntax.mkApp functionRef (#[stateBinder] ++ carried))))\n    let lambda ← carried.foldrM (init := body) fun binder inner =>\n      `(term| fun $binder:ident => $inner)\n    let resultType ← `(term| List (Umpire.Step $stateIdent $outcomeIdent $factIdent))\n    let functionType ← (entry.inputFields.map Prod.snd).foldrM (init := resultType)\n      fun domain inner => `(term| $(mkIdent (`_root_ ++ domain)) → $inner)\n    let functionName := mkIdentFrom name (name.getId ++ `derived ++ Name.mkSimple entry.name)\n    elabGenerated (← `(command|\n      def $functionName : $stateIdent → $functionType := fun $stateBinder:ident => $lambda))\n    steps := steps.push {\n      action := entry\n      domains := entry.inputFields.map Prod.snd\n      function := functionName }\n  -- Each extension against the source, over every state and every member of the source's Action\n  -- catalog; the other actions' members extend by nothing.\n  for (spelling, actionRef, functionRef) in extensions do\n    let arms ← sourceSteps.mapM fun entry => do\n      let carried := carriedOf entry\n      if entry.name == spelling then\n        `(Lean.Parser.Term.matchAltExpr|\n          | $(Syntax.mkApp (sourceIdent (`Action ++ Name.mkSimple entry.name)) carried) =>\n              $(Syntax.mkApp functionRef (#[stateBinder] ++ carried)))\n      else\n        let holes ← carried.mapM fun _ => `(term| _)\n        `(Lean.Parser.Term.matchAltExpr|\n          | $(Syntax.mkApp (sourceIdent (`Action ++ Name.mkSimple entry.name)) holes) => [])\n    let conflict ← `(term|\n      ((Umpire.Command.Derived.firstConflict (Umpire.Command.members (α := $stateIdent))\n          $(sourceIdent `actions) $(sourceIdent `stateKeyFor) $(sourceIdent `actionKeyFor)\n          $(sourceIdent `outcomeKeyFor) $(sourceIdent `factKeyFor) $(sourceIdent `step)\n          (fun $stateBinder:ident taken => match taken with $arms:matchAlt*)).map\n        Umpire.Command.Derived.ExtendConflict.fields).getD [])\n    match ← liftTermElabM (evalStringListTerm conflict) with\n    | [\"disabledSource\", state, action] =>\n        throwErrorAt actionRef (extendDisabledMessage spelling state action)\n    | [\"duplicate\", state, action] =>\n        throwErrorAt actionRef (extendDuplicateMessage spelling state action)\n    | _ => pure ()\n  -- A restriction keeps no timer, since `restrict:` names none.\n  let timers := if restrictRefs.isEmpty then source.timers else #[]\n  pure { source, sourceModel, entity, steps, timers }\n\nelab doc?:(docComment)? machineKeyword name:ident keys:machineKey+ : command => do\n  -- A machine's generated definitions are as long as its state space: `DESIGN.md` section 3's\n  -- protocol machine has 224 states, so its key array, its terminal list and its enumerated table\n  -- are lists of that length, and building a list term that long recurses deeper than a Lean file\n  -- normally does. The depth is raised for what this command generates and nothing else, because\n  -- the length is the machine's size rather than anything an author wrote.\n  let mut entity : Option Registry.EntityEntry := none\n  let mut stateRef : Option Ident := none\n  let mut endRefs : Array Ident := #[]\n  let mut startRefs : Array Ident := #[]\n  let mut timerRefs : Array Ident := #[]\n  let mut unobservableRefs : Array Ident := #[]\n  let mut setupParameters : Array (String × Name) := #[]\n  let mut evidenceRefs : Array (Ident × Ident) := #[]\n  let mut stepRefs : Array (Ident × Ident) := #[]\n  let mut refinesRef : Option Ident := none\n  let mut mapRef : Option Ident := none\n  let mut fromRef : Option Ident := none\n  let mut restrictEntry : Option Syntax := none\n  let mut restrictRefs : Array Ident := #[]\n  let mut extendEntry : Option Syntax := none\n  let mut extendRefs : Array (Ident × Ident) := #[]\n  -- The keys a derived machine takes from its source, so a `from:` line can refuse the first.\n  let mut authoredKeys : Array Syntax := #[]\n  for entry in keys do\n    match entry with\n    | `(machineKey| from: $sourceRef:ident) => do\n        if fromRef.isSome then throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"from:\")\n        fromRef := some sourceRef\n    | `(machineKey| restrict: [$members,*]) => do\n        if restrictEntry.isSome then throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"restrict:\")\n        restrictEntry := some entry\n        restrictRefs := members.getElems\n    | `(machineKey| extend: $[$extended:ident : $extension:ident]*) => do\n        if extendEntry.isSome then throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"extend:\")\n        extendEntry := some entry\n        for takenOn in extended, function? in extension do\n          extendRefs := extendRefs.push (takenOn, function?)\n    | _ => authoredKeys := authoredKeys.push entry\n  if fromRef.isSome then\n    if let some entry := authoredKeys[0]? then throwErrorAt entry derivedOwnKeyMessage\n  else\n    if let some entry := restrictEntry then\n      throwErrorAt entry (derivationWithoutFromMessage \"restrict:\")\n    if let some entry := extendEntry then\n      throwErrorAt entry (derivationWithoutFromMessage \"extend:\")\n  for entry in keys do\n    match entry with\n    | `(machineKey| from: $_:ident) | `(machineKey| restrict: [$_,*]) => pure ()\n    | `(machineKey| extend: $[$_:ident : $_:ident]*) => pure ()\n    | `(machineKey| refines: $refinedRef:ident) => do\n        if refinesRef.isSome then throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"refines:\")\n        refinesRef := some refinedRef\n    | `(machineKey| map: $mapFn:ident) => do\n        if mapRef.isSome then throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"map:\")\n        mapRef := some mapFn\n    | `(machineKey| for: $entityRef:ident) => do\n        if entity.isSome then throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"for:\")\n        let declName? ← try\n            some <$> liftTermElabM (realizeGlobalConstNoOverloadWithInfo entityRef)\n          catch failure =>\n            if failure.isInterrupt || failure.isMaxRecDepth then throw failure else pure none\n        match declName?.bind (Registry.entity? (← getEnv)) with\n        | some declared => entity := some declared\n        | none => throwErrorAt entityRef (undeclaredMachineEntityMessage entityRef.getId)\n    | `(machineKey| state: $typeRef:ident) => do\n        if stateRef.isSome then throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"state:\")\n        stateRef := some typeRef\n    | `(machineKey| ends: [$members,*]) => do\n        if !endRefs.isEmpty then throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"ends:\")\n        endRefs := members.getElems\n    -- The antiquotation names avoid `actions`, because `actions:` is already a token and\n    -- `$actions:ident` would tokenize as `$` and that token rather than as an antiquotation.\n    | `(machineKey| timers: [$members,*]) => do\n        if !timerRefs.isEmpty then throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"timers:\")\n        timerRefs := members.getElems\n    | `(machineKey| unobservable: [$members,*]) => do\n        if !unobservableRefs.isEmpty then\n          throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"unobservable:\")\n        unobservableRefs := members.getElems\n    | `(machineKey| setup: $[$parameter:ident : $domain:ident]*) => do\n        if !setupParameters.isEmpty then throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"setup:\")\n        for named in parameter, ranged in domain do\n          setupParameters := setupParameters.push\n            (named.getId.toString, ← resolveSetupDomain ranged)\n    -- `recorded`, not `fact`: `fact:` is already a token of the `property` command's `require:`\n    -- block,\n    -- so `$fact:ident` tokenizes as `$` and that token rather than as an antiquotation. The same\n    -- trap as `$actions:ident`, one category over.\n    | `(machineKey| evidence: $[$recorded:ident : $observed:ident]*) => do\n        if !evidenceRefs.isEmpty then throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"evidence:\")\n        for named in recorded, seen in observed do\n          evidenceRefs := evidenceRefs.push (named, seen)\n    | `(machineKey| starts: [$members,*]) => do\n        if !startRefs.isEmpty then throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"starts:\")\n        startRefs := members.getElems\n    -- The antiquotation names avoid `actions`, because `actions:` is already a token and\n    -- `$actions:ident` would tokenize as `$` and that token rather than as an antiquotation.\n    | `(machineKey| steps: $[$stepped:ident : $written:ident]*) => do\n        if !stepRefs.isEmpty then throwErrorAt entry (duplicateKeyMessage \"machine\" \"steps:\")\n        for takenOn in stepped, function? in written do\n          stepRefs := stepRefs.push (takenOn, function?)\n    | _ => throwErrorAt entry \"unsupported machine key\"\n  -- A derived machine is the source's entity, state and steps, so everything below reads it as if\n  -- the author had written them, except where the source's own lines are values rather than\n  -- spellings: its starts, ends, setup and evidence, which are carried over further down.\n  let derivation ← fromRef.mapM fun sourceRef =>\n    deriveMachine name sourceRef restrictRefs extendRefs\n  if let some derived := derivation then\n    entity := some derived.entity\n    stateRef := some (mkIdentFrom name (`_root_ ++ derived.source.stateType))\n    evidenceRefs := derived.source.evidence.map fun (recorded, observed) =>\n      (mkIdentFrom name (Name.mkSimple recorded), mkIdentFrom name (Name.mkSimple observed))\n  let some declaredEntity := entity\n    | throwErrorAt name (missingKeyMessage \"machine\" \"for:\")\n  let some stateType := stateRef\n    | throwErrorAt name (missingKeyMessage \"machine\" \"state:\")\n  if derivation.isNone then\n    if stepRefs.isEmpty then throwErrorAt name (missingKeyMessage \"machine\" \"steps:\")\n    -- Without `starts:` a Model begins nowhere, so nothing is reachable, every Property holds\n    -- vacuously and the stuck check passes by having nothing to check.\n    if startRefs.isEmpty then throwErrorAt name (missingKeyMessage \"machine\" \"starts:\")\n    -- Without `ends:` no state is terminal: `terminal` names nothing, a Search runs to its limit on\n    -- every path, the stuck check has no state it is allowed to stop in, and a Property that\n    -- requires an instance to finish holds by never being reached. A machine says where it ends.\n    if endRefs.isEmpty then throwErrorAt name (missingKeyMessage \"machine\" \"ends:\")\n  -- A refinement is a `refines:` and a `map:` together: the machine refined, and the function that\n  -- reads this machine's state as its state. Neither says anything without the other.\n  let refinement ← match refinesRef, mapRef with\n    | none, none => pure none\n    | some refinedRef, none => throwErrorAt refinedRef refinesWithoutMapMessage\n    | none, some mapFn => throwErrorAt mapFn mapWithoutRefinesMessage\n    let outcomePairs ← pairTerms\n      (sameNamedPairs (outcomeMembers.map (·.key)) refinedModel.outcomes.toList)\n    let factPairs ← pairTerms (sameNamedPairs (factMembers.map (·.key)) refinedModel.facts.toList)\n    let refinedOutcomeType := mkIdent refinedModel.outcomeType\n    let refinedFactType := mkIdent refinedModel.factType\n    let abstractionName := mkIdentFrom name (name.getId ++ `abstraction)\n    elabGenerated (← `(command|\n      def $abstractionName : Umpire.RefinementMorphism $setupType $stateType\n          $(mkIdent outcomeType) $(mkIdent factType)\n          $(mkIdent (refinedMachine.declName ++ `Setup)) $(mkIdent refinedModel.stateType)\n          $refinedOutcomeType $refinedFactType :=\n        Umpire.Command.refinementMorphism ($refined).setupValue $mapFn\n          $outcomeKeyForName (Umpire.Command.members (α := $refinedOutcomeType))\n          $(mkIdent (refinedMachine.declName ++ `outcomeKeyFor)) [$outcomePairs,*]\n          $factKeyForName (Umpire.Command.members (α := $refinedFactType))\n          $(mkIdent (refinedMachine.declName ++ `factKeyFor)) [$factPairs,*]))\n    let reportName := mkIdentFrom name (name.getId ++ `refinement)\n    elabGenerated (← `(command|\n      def $reportName : Umpire.Command.RefinementReport :=\n        Umpire.Command.deriveRefinement ($name) ($refined) $abstractionName\n          $(Lean.quote name.getId.toString) $(Lean.quote refinedMachine.name)))\n    let report ← liftTermElabM (evalRefinementReport ((← getCurrNamespace) ++ reportName.getId))\n    if let some rejected := report.rejected then\n      throwErrorAt mapFn rejected\n    let witnessName := mkIdentFrom name (name.getId ++ `refines)\n    elabGenerated (← `(command|\n      theorem $witnessName :\n          Umpire.TableRefinement ($name).table ($refined).table $abstractionName :=\n        Umpire.TableRefinement.ofChecked (by decide +kernel)))\n    let witness := (← getCurrNamespace) ++ witnessName.getId\n    let decided ← if (← getEnv).contains witness then\n        pure !(← liftCoreM (Lean.collectAxioms witness)).contains ``sorryAx\n      else pure false\n    unless decided do\n      throwErrorAt refinedRef (undecidedRefinementMessage report.rows.length)\n  -- A machine declares a Model, so `property`, `scenario` and `query` have to see one. What they\n  -- resolve members by is the key the table carries -- a state's key names every field of the\n  -- structure -- because that is what the emitted rows, the Behavior Fingerprint and a Contract all\n  -- read. A machine over a one-field state keeps the bare spellings a `model` had.\n  liftCoreM (Registry.recordModel {\n    declName := declared\n    role := declaredEntity.name\n    stateType := stateDecl\n    actionType := actionDecl\n    outcomeType\n    factType\n    «states» := (stateMembers.map (·.key)).toArray\n    «actions» := (actionMembers.map (·.key)).toArray\n    «outcomes» := (outcomeMembers.map (·.key)).toArray\n    «facts» := (factMembers.map (·.key)).toArray\n    «starts» := startKeys })\n  liftCoreM (Registry.recordMachine {\n    declName := (← getCurrNamespace) ++ name.getId\n    name := name.getId.toString\n    id := ← definitionIdHere \"machine\" name.getId.toString\n    entity := declaredEntity.declName\n    stateType := stateDecl\n    steps := steps.map fun resolved => (resolved.action.name, resolved.function.getId)\n    actionDecls := steps.filterMap fun resolved =>\n      if resolved.action.declName.isAnonymous then none else some resolved.action.declName\n    timers := timerNames\n    unobservable := unobservableNames\n    evidence := evidenceRefs.map fun (recordedRef, observedRef) =>\n      (recordedRef.getId.getString!, observedRef.getId.getString!)\n    refines := refinement.map fun (_, refinedMachine, _, _) => refinedMachine.declName\n    abstraction := match refinement with\n      | some (_, _, _, mapFn) => mapFn.getId\n      | none => .anonymous })\n\n\n/-! ### The compose command\n\nA composition is one Model built from several machines: the author's state structure has one field\nper member, `members:` says which machine each field holds, and `sync:` says which members' actions\nare one step. It defines no behavior of its own. Every row is a combination of member rows, walked\nby `Umpire.Command.Compose` from the composed start states and emitted as a literal table, so what a\nQuery searches is what the member tables authorize together.\n\nThe Action, Outcome and Fact domains are generated as tagged unions with one constructor per member\nfield, wrapping that member's own type, and one Action constructor per `sync:` line: two members'\ncatalogs share keys -- `accepted` twice -- and the union keeps them apart, so a predicate writes\n`step.facts.contains (.job .replied)`. The state structure is the author's, because a predicate\nreads its field names; it derives no `Finite`, and the states the composition reaches are its\ncatalog. -/\n\n/-- One `sync:` line: the synchronized action's name and its participants, each `field.action`. -/\nsyntax composeSync := ident \":\" sepBy1(ident, \" ∥ \")\n\n/-- One indented key of a `compose` declaration. -/\ndeclare_syntax_cat composeKey\nsyntax \"for:\" \"[\" ident,+ \"]\" : composeKey\nsyntax \"state:\" ident : composeKey\nsyntax \"members:\" withPosition((colGe ident \":\" ident)+) : composeKey\nsyntax \"sync:\" withPosition((colGe composeSync)+) : composeKey\nsyntax \"starts:\" \"[\" ident,+ \"]\" : composeKey\nsyntax \"ends:\" \"[\" ident,+ \"]\" : composeKey\n\n@[run_parser_attribute_hooks] private def composeKeyword := declarationKeyword \"compose\"\n\nprivate def composedStateMessage (spelling : Name) : String :=\n  s!\"'{spelling}' is not a structure; a composition's `state:` names a structure with one field per \\\nmember, whose field names are what a predicate reads\"\n\nprivate def memberFieldUnderscoreMessage (field : String) : String :=\n  s!\"member field '{field}' contains '_'; a composed key joins a member's own key to its field with \\\n`_`, so a field name carries none\"\n\nprivate def notAStateFieldMessage (field : String) (fields : List String) : String :=\n  s!\"'{field}' is not a field of the composition's state structure; its fields are \\\n{\", \".intercalate fields}\"\n\nprivate def unlistedFieldMessage (field : String) : String :=\n  s!\"the state structure's field '{field}' has no `members:` line; every field holds one member\"\n\nprivate def memberNotMachineMessage (spelling : Name) : String :=\n  s!\"'{spelling}' is not a machine declared by a `machine` command; each member is a machine\"\n\nprivate def memberStateMessage (field : String) (machine expected : Name) (found : Expr) :\n    MessageData :=\n  m!\"the field '{field}' is {found}, and '{machine}' keeps {expected}; a member field holds its \\\nmachine's state\"\n\nprivate def untrackedEntityMessage (machine entity : Name) : String :=\n  s!\"'{machine}' tracks '{entity}', which `for:` does not name; `for:` names every entity the \\\nmembers track\"\n\nprivate def unusedEntityMessage (entity : String) : String :=\n  s!\"no member tracks '{entity}'; `for:` names the entities the members track\"\n\nprivate def composedValueMessage (spelling : String) (fields : List String) : String :=\n  s!\"'{spelling}' names no value of a member field; `starts:` and `ends:` name `field.value`, one \\\nof the member fields {\", \".intercalate fields} and a value its machine's state holds\"\n\nprivate def unstartedValueMessage (spelling : String) : String :=\n  s!\"no start state of the member holds '{spelling}'; a composition starts each member where its \\\nmachine starts\"\n\nprivate def participantShapeMessage (spelling : String) : String :=\n  s!\"'{spelling}' is not `field.action`; a `sync:` participant names a member field and one of its \\\nactions\"\n\nprivate def syncNameMessage (spelling : String) : String :=\n  s!\"'{spelling}' is already a member field or `sync:` name; each synchronized action is named once \\\nand apart from the members\"\n\nprivate def neverEnabledMessage (key : String) : String :=\n  s!\"'{key}' is never enabled: no composed state the composition reaches has a row for it, so its \\\ncatalog leaves it out\"\n\nprivate def disagreeingLiteralMessage (spelling : String) (states actions : Nat) : String :=\n  s!\"the literal table of '{spelling}', {states} states over {actions} actions, is not the \\\ncomposition of its members over the states its starts reach, so the kernel refused its agreement \\\ntheorem; the walk emitted a row the members do not authorize, missed one they do, or kept a state \\\nno start reaches\"\n\nprivate unsafe def evalIndexedUnsafe (α : Type) (type : Expr) (term : Term) :\n    Elab.Term.TermElabM α := do\n  let value ← Elab.Term.elabTermEnsuringType term type\n  Elab.Term.synthesizeSyntheticMVarsNoPostponing\n  Meta.evalExpr α type (← instantiateMVars value)\n\n/-- A term's value of `type`, computed by the compiler. {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_16","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/zsh -lc \"sed -n '920,1210p' model/AUTHORING.md\"","aggregated_output":"/- Every reply, of any class, leaves the handler's worker polling: no handler replies while its\nworker is stopped. -/\nproperty repliedByPollingWorker\n  machine: nexusCaller\n  when: handlerReply\n  holds: fun step => step.state.worker.phase == .polling\n\n/- A retryable reply backs the operation off; the handler's worker then stops, so the retried\nattempt is never answered and the schedule-to-start deadline fires. -/\nscenario repliedThenStopped\n  model: nexusCaller\n  starts: operation.unscheduled\n  actions: [operation.schedule (unset, expires, unset), handlerReply (handlerError true),\n    workerStop, operation.scheduleToStart]\n\nquery stoppedWorkerRepliesNothing\n  verify: repliedByPollingWorker\n  in: repliedThenStopped\n  limits: four\n```\n\n## 13. The worker entity, a second composition, and a derived machine\n\nSection 12's composition names the worker entity's module. This section quotes that module, the\nOutage Model's composition and the derived negative control, and the drift test checks their marked\nregions as it checks this file's: a region name is unique across every file the walkthrough quotes,\nand each file closes its last quoted region with its own `-- authoring: end`.\n\n**An entity module.** [`Temporal/Feature/Worker/Model.lean`](Temporal/Feature/Worker/Model.lean)\ndeclares one entity, the worker of a task queue, and no set, Case or Query: nothing in it is\nrealized on its own. Its machine is what a composition synchronizes with. The handler's worker and\nthe workflow's worker are two instances of it, and the composition that uses one names it by its\nmember field.\n\n<!-- authoring: worker -->\n```lean\n/-! ### Entities and domains -/\n\n/-- A worker is named by the task queue it polls: the handler's worker and the workflow's worker are\ntwo instances of this entity, told apart by their queue. -/\nentity worker\n  key: taskQueue\n\nenum Phase\n  | polling\n  | stopped\n\nstructure WorkerState where\n  phase : Phase\n  deriving BEq, DecidableEq, Repr, Finite\n\nenum WorkerOutcome\n  | accepted\n\n/-- A worker records nothing of its own: its stop and resume are faults the Run records against no\nentity, and what it serves is recorded by the work it serves. -/\ninductive WorkerFact\n  deriving BEq, DecidableEq, Repr, Finite\n\n/-! ### The actions\n\nThe two faults are the `worker` party's and name no entity, as the outage machine spells them. The\nserve action is the worker's own and takes no input, so a composition may synchronize it with an\naction of any class. -/\n\naction workerStop\n  party: worker\n\naction workerResume\n  party: worker\n\naction serve\n  party: worker\n  on: worker\n```\n\nThe machine starts polling and may end in either phase, because a worker has no natural end. A\npolling worker serves; a stopped one has no `serve` row, which is the whole point of composing it.\n\n<!-- authoring: polling -->\n```lean\n/-! ### The machine -/\n\n/-- A polling worker stops; a stopped one has nothing to stop. -/\ndef stopStep (state : WorkerState) :\n    List (Step WorkerState WorkerOutcome WorkerFact) :=\n  if state.phase != .polling then [] else\n  [{ outcome := .accepted, state := { phase := .stopped }, facts := [] }]\n\n/-- A stopped worker resumes polling; a polling one has nothing to resume. -/\ndef resumeStep (state : WorkerState) :\n    List (Step WorkerState WorkerOutcome WorkerFact) :=\n  if state.phase != .stopped then [] else\n  [{ outcome := .accepted, state := { phase := .polling }, facts := [] }]\n\n/-- A polling worker serves and keeps polling; a stopped one serves nothing. -/\ndef serveStep (state : WorkerState) :\n    List (Step WorkerState WorkerOutcome WorkerFact) :=\n  if state.phase != .polling then [] else\n  [{ outcome := .accepted, state, facts := [] }]\n\n/-- A worker has no natural end: it may be left polling or stopped. -/\nmachine polling\n  for: worker\n  state: WorkerState\n  starts: [polling]\n  ends: [polling, stopped]\n  steps:\n    workerStop: stopStep\n    workerResume: resumeStep\n    serve: serveStep\n```\n\nAn entity or action name is declared once across the production modules under\n`Temporal.Feature`: `make lint-model` fails a second declaration and names both modules\n(`feature-entity-uniqueness`, drafted as MOD-18). Its allowlist, in `model/ModelLint/Entity.lean`,\nholds the duplicates that remain once the worker module exists (`workflow` in the caller, Start and\nOutage modules, `startWorkflow` in Start and Outage, `workerStop` in the caller, Outage and worker\nmodules, `workerResume` in Outage and worker), each removed by the follow-up that lets a use case\nchoose its entity key and moves the operation entity. Test modules and the success specimen are\nout of scope.\n\n**A second composition.** The Outage Model\n([`Temporal/Feature/Workflow/Outage/Model.lean`](Temporal/Feature/Workflow/Outage/Model.lean))\ncomposes its unchanged machine with the full `polling` machine. All three worker actions are\nsynchronized: the two faults are one step of the workflow's stutter row and the worker's phase\nchange, and the wait for completion is the worker serving. The claim fixes the one composed state a\ncompletion leaves, so it holds as a whole-state requirement; the caller's claim in section 12 fixes\none member's field while the other varies, which is the field-addressed requirement.\n\n<!-- authoring: outage -->\n```lean\n/-! ### The workflow and its worker\n\nThe outage machine composed with the worker of the workflow's task queue. The two faults are one\nstep of both: the workflow's stutter row and the worker's phase change. The wait is the worker\nserving the workflow task, so it has a row only while the worker polls, and the workflow completes\nin no state where the worker is stopped. The functional set stays over the machine alone; the\ncomposition is what the cross-entity claim is verified over. -/\n\nstructure WorkerOutageState where\n  workflow : OutageState\n  worker : Worker.WorkerState\n  deriving BEq, DecidableEq, Repr\n\ncompose workerOutage\n  for: [workflow, Worker.worker]\n  state: WorkerOutageState\n  members:\n    workflow: workflowOutage\n    worker: Worker.polling\n  sync:\n    workerStop: workflow.workerStop ∥ worker.workerStop\n    workerResume: workflow.workerResume ∥ worker.workerResume\n    awaitCompletion: workflow.awaitCompletion ∥ worker.serve\n  starts: [workflow.pending, worker.polling]\n  ends: [workflow.completed]\n\n/- A completion leaves the one composed state in which the workflow is completed and its worker is\npolling, and records the completed event: no workflow completes while its worker is stopped. -/\nproperty completedByPollingWorker\n  machine: workerOutage\n  when: awaitCompletion\n  holds: fun step =>\n    step.state.workflow.phase == .completed && step.state.worker.phase == .polling &&\n      step.facts.contains (.workflow .workflowExecutionCompleted)\n\n/- The outage over the composition: the stop and the resume move the worker, and the wait is\nserved by the resumed worker. -/\nscenario outageServed\n  model: workerOutage\n  starts: workflow.pending\n  actions: [workerStop, workflow.startWorkflow, workerResume, awaitCompletion]\n\nquery stoppedWorkerCompletesNothing\n  verify: completedByPollingWorker\n  in: outageServed\n  limits: four\n```\n\nWhat `compose` generates, for both compositions:\n\n- `workerOutage.Action`, `.Outcome` and `.Fact`, tagged unions with one constructor per member\n  wrapping that member's type, which is why a predicate writes\n  `step.facts.contains (.workflow .workflowExecutionCompleted)`.\n- Catalog keys a fixture and a Definition ID can carry: a composed state `_`-joins its members'\n  state keys in field order (`completed_polling`), an unsynchronized action is `<field>_<key>`\n  (`workflow_startWorkflow`), and a synchronized action is its `sync:` name. Member timers,\n  unobservable entries and evidence lines are lifted under the same `<field>_<key>` spelling. A\n  member's state fields are lowered as `<field>_<memberField>` (`<field>` for a one-field member),\n  which is what a field-addressed requirement fixes. Definition IDs hang off the owner\n  `compose-<name>` under the file's namespace.\n- The table of reachable rows only: a breadth-first walk from the starts over the members' tables,\n  every catalog and each row's results sorted by the lowered order key, emitted as a literal. The\n  walk refuses a composition whose reachable states times actions exceed the enumeration bound,\n  with both counts. `workerOutage` reaches 6 states; `nexusCaller` reaches 316 and 1468 rows.\n- `<name>.agrees`, a theorem decided by `decide +kernel` that the literal agrees with the\n  composition of the members' tables over every state the starts reach: rows grouped by source\n  state, each member table read once. A literal the kernel refuses is a located error. On\n  `nexusCaller` the decisions take 56.5 s of kernel time and the whole caller Model file 241 s at\n  6.8 GB, which is the cost of composing a 158-state machine.\n\nA step of an unsynchronized action moves its member and leaves the others; a `sync:` step is\nenabled only where every participant has a row, and its results are the ordered product of theirs.\nTwo members owning an action of one name that no `sync:` line pairs is a located error, and so is a\n`sync:` line naming a timer. The composition carries no `refines:` and no abstract field. No set\nnames it: `set` over a composition is refused, so its Queries are `verify` Queries, and\n`Selection.select` runs both shipped ones on `veil` with reason `default`.\n\n**A derived machine.** A `machine` with `from:` takes its source's entity, state type, starts, ends,\nsetup and evidence, and its rows through the source's own step functions; it carries neither\n`refines:` nor the abstract field, and it generates its own Action catalog, so its Definition IDs\nare its own. `restrict: [actions]` keeps the listed actions' rows and drops the rest, every timer\nand unobservable entry, and every evidence line for a fact no kept row records (section 12's\n`handlerWorker`). `extend:` adds an author's results to an action's rows, sorted into the step order\nkey; a result where the source has no row, or one the source already returns, is a located error.\nBoth may appear on one machine, and `restrict:` applies first. The negative control\n([`Temporal/Feature/Nexus/Control/Model.lean`](Temporal/Feature/Nexus/Control/Model.lean)) is the\npair machine plus one forged result, so one function is its only step code, and its catalogs, its\nCase fixture and its recorded Run are the ones the copied machine had.\n\n<!-- authoring: derived -->\n```lean\n/-! ### The machine\n\nThe pair Model's machine -- the operation without its deadlines and retries -- plus the forged row.\nThe machine is derived from it rather than copied: every row is the pair machine's, and the one\nfunction written here returns the one result the platform never takes. -/\n\n/-- The forged row: the non-retryable error completes the operation and records the completed\nevent. The platform's row for that error, which fails the operation and records the failed event,\nis the pair machine's and stays beside it. -/\ndef controlForgedStep (state : PairState) (reply : Reply) :\n    List (Step PairState PairOutcome PairFact) :=\n  if state.phase != .scheduled then [] else\n  match reply with\n  | .handlerError false =>\n      [{ outcome := .accepted, state := { phase := .succeeded },\n         facts := [.nexusOperationCompleted] }]\n  | _ => []\n\nmachine nexusControl\n  from: pair\n  extend:\n    handlerReply: controlForgedStep\n```\n\n## 14. Importing them directly, rather than an aggregate, is what reaches a feature module nothing\nelse imports. -/\ndef importRoots (sources : Array Lean.Name) : Array Lean.Name :=\n  (sources.filter inScope).qsort nameLt\n\n/-- Every in-scope declaration whose name another in-scope module of the same kind declares. A block is one list item or\n// one blank-line-separated paragraph, so a rule marked planned covers the names in its\n// own text and no others.\nfunc ExtractSpecNames(document string) []SpecName {\n\tlines := strings.Split(document, \"\\n\")\n\tblockOf := make([]int, len(lines))\n\tblock := 0\n\tpreviousBlank := true\n\tfor index, line := range lines {\n\t\tblank := strings.TrimSpace(line) == \"\"\n\t\tif blockStart.MatchString(line) || (previousBlank && !blank) {\n\t\t\tblock++\n\t\t}\n\t\tblockOf[index] = block\n\t\tpreviousBlank = blank\n\t}\n\n\tplannedOf := map[int]string{}\n\tfor index, line := range lines {\n\t\tif match := plannedMarker.FindStringSubmatch(line); match != nil {\n\t\t\tplannedOf[blockOf[index]] = match[1]\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tvar names []SpecName\n\tfor index, line := range lines {\n\t\tfor _, match := range backticked.FindAllStringSubmatch(line, -1) {\n\t\t\tcandidate := match[1]\n\t\t\tif !modelName.MatchString(candidate) {\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tnames = append(names, SpecName{\n\t\t\t\tName:        candidate,\n\t\t\t\tLine:        index + 1,\n\t\t\t\tPlannedSpec: plannedOf[blockOf[index]],\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn names\n}\n\n// Unresolved returns one message per citation a document makes that the tree does not\n// back: a name that is not in the index, or a name marked planned whose owning Flow spec\n// is not open. Messages are sorted, and an empty result means the document's mechanical\n// half holds. `label` prefixes each message, so a caller can name the document.\nfunc Unresolved(index *Index, names []SpecName, label, specsDirectory string) ([]string, error) {\n\topenSpec := map[string]bool{}\n\tvar messages []string\n\tfor _, name := range names {\n\t\t// A name that resolves is accepted whatever its block says.
